Deep immune cell phenotyping predicts influenza vaccine immunogenicity in HCT recipients

To fix (2)

To fix Exact P-Values

What we found

We found 3 imprecise p values out of 23 detected p values.

Show details

Reporting p values imprecisely (e.g., p < .05) reduces transparency, reproducibility, and re-use (e.g., in p value meta-analyses). Best practice is to report exact p-values with three decimal places (e.g., p = .032) unless p values are smaller than 0.001, in which case you can use p < .001.

This module sometimes flags false positives when sentences discuss how p-value thresholds were set. It can also miss an imprecise p-value that is reported against a threshold (e.g., “decreased at the p < .05 significance threshold”).

P-Value Text
P ≤ .001 Adding immune-cell composition to baseline titers raised the AUC for a fourfold composite rise from 0.75 to 0.80 (difference, 0.050; 95% CI, 0.013-0.089; training-aware P ≤ .001, DeLong P = .009; Figure 4D), and adding it to titers and clinical factors raised the AUC from 0.78 to 0.81 (P = .034).
P ≤ .001 The Spearman rank correlation between the score and the observed composite fold-rise was 0.39 (P ≤ .001), and the same gradient appeared for each antigen separately (supplemental Figures S10, S11 and S12; supplemental Table S4).
P ≤ .001 The odds ratio per SD was 1.8 in children and 3.1 in adults (interaction P = .10), the rank correlation with the observed fold-rise was 0.37 (P = .002) and 0.50 (P ≤ .001), and fourfold rises were least frequent in the lowest fifth in both (22% and 10%; Figure 5B and C).

The APA manual states: Report exact p values (e.g., p = .031) to two or three decimal places. However, report p values less than .001 as p < .001. However, 2 decimals is too imprecise for many use-cases (e.g., a p value meta-analysis), so report p values with three digits.

American Psychological Association (2020). Publication manual of the American Psychological Association, 7 edition. American Psychological Association.

To check (2)

To check Non-Significant P Value Check

What we found

We found 7 non-significant p values that should be checked for appropriate interpretation.

Show details

Meta-scientific research has shown nonsignificant p values are commonly misinterpreted. It is incorrect to infer that there is ‘no effect’, ‘no difference’, or that groups are ‘the same’ after p > 0.05.

It is possible that there is a true non-zero effect, but that the study did not detect it. Make sure your inference acknowledges that it is possible that there is a non-zero effect. It is correct to include the effect is ‘not significantly’ different, although this just restates that p > 0.05.

Metacheck does not yet analyze automatically whether sentences which include non-significant p-values are correct, but we recommend manually checking the sentences below for possible misinterpreted non-significant p values.

Text Sentence
P = .072 Clinical factors alone reduced MSE by 6.0% (M1→M2; P = .072), and adding immune-cell composition on top of them still improved prediction by 12.5% (M2→M4; 4.6-20.3%; P = .001).
P = .20 Adding clinical factors to a model that already had cellular composition gave 3.0% (P = .20), and the two information sources together improved prediction by 17.8% over titers alone (M1→M4; 8.7-26.5%; P = .001).
P = .15 The pooled model reduced prediction error beyond baseline titers by 9.4% in children (P = .15) and 22.9% in adults (P < .001), so the pooled model improved prediction in both, more in adults.
P = .23 Refitting within each cohort on the shared 35-population atlas reduced prediction error by 6.2% in children (n = 147; P = .23) and 25.9% in adults (n = 113; P < .001), with wide intervals in each cohort; gains by baseline-titer quartile within each cohort are in supplemental Figure S15.
P = .10 The odds ratio per SD was 1.8 in children and 3.1 in adults (interaction P = .10), the rank correlation with the observed fold-rise was 0.37 (P = .002) and 0.50 (P ≤ .001), and fourfold rises were least frequent in the lowest fifth in both (22% and 10%; Figure 5B and C).
P = .20 Adding immune-cell composition to baseline titers reduced the prediction error of the composite fold-rise in these episodes by 39.2% (95% recipient-bootstrap CI, 28.3-48.9%), and the AUC for a fourfold composite rise rose from 0.65 with titers alone to 0.71 with composition (difference, 0.06; 95% CI, -0.03 to 0.14; DeLong P = .20;
P = .086 Each SD of the first-season-standardized score was associated with 1.8-fold higher odds of a fourfold rise (95% CI, 0.9-3.5; P = .086).

For metascientific articles demonstrating the rate of misinterpretations of non-significant results is high, see:

Aczel B, Palfi B, Szollosi A, Kovacs M, Szaszi B, Szecsi P, Zrubka M, Gronau Q, van den Bergh D, Wagenmakers E (2018). “Quantifying Support for the Null Hypothesis in Psychology: An Empirical Investigation.” Advances in Methods and Practices in Psychological Science, 1(3), 357–366. doi:10.1177/2515245918773742.

Murphy S, Merz R, Reimann L, Fernández A (2025). “Nonsignificance misinterpreted as an effect’s absence in psychology: Prevalence and temporal analyses.” Royal Society Open Science, 12(3), 242167. doi:10.1098/rsos.242167.

For educational material on preventing the misinterpretation of p values, see Improving Your Statistical Inferences.

Working Directory (setwd)

Best programming practice is to avoid setwd() in analysis code: it hardcodes an assumption about the working directory (often an absolute path on the author’s own machine), so the code breaks when run anywhere else. Keep the working directory as the caller sets it and use relative paths. No setwd() calls were found in any of the code files.

Package Installation (install.packages)

Best programming practice is to avoid install.packages() in analysis code: it installs software on whoever runs the script without asking, which is at minimum disruptive (an unexpected install, network access, a package version the author never tested against) and at worst a security risk if it points at an untrusted repository. Document required packages instead (a README, or a list of them: an renv.lock/DESCRIPTION for R, a requirements.txt for Python). No install.packages() calls were found in any of the code files.

Libraries / Imports

Best programming practice is to load all required libraries/imports in one block near the top of the code. In 10 code files, libraries/imports were at multiple places (i.e., with more than 3 non-comment lines in between).

Packages / Dependencies

The code files load 39 distinct packages/libraries: BiocManager, caret, ConsensusClusterPlus, csv, data.table, digest, doParallel, FlowSOM, FNN, foreach, ggplot2, ggrepel, glmnet, grDevices, here, igraph, knitr, lmtest, MASS, math, os, pandas, parallel, patchwork, pathlib, pROC, randomForest, ranger, RANN, RColorBrewer, re, rjson, sandwich, scales, stats, tools, utils, uwot, xgboost. These are the names found in the source (no version information is available from static analysis).

Reproducible Environment

No renv.lock file, sessionInfo()/session_info() record, or groundhog/checkpoint date-pin call was found anywhere in the repository. Without one of these, the exact R and package versions used for the analysis are not recoverable, and package versions may drift between when this analysis was run and any later reproduction attempt. Consider depositing an renv.lock (via the renv package), a sessionInfo() text dump, or a groundhog/checkpoint date-pin alongside the code.

The code should be changed, because as written it does not find these files. 59 data paths were built at runtime with sprintf()/paste()/file.path() instead of written as a literal path — this is fragile authoring practice (it silently breaks if the code is run from a different working directory than the author’s own). We resolved all of these on a best-effort basis.

Execution

The code was run. Each script ran in an isolated Docker container against a throwaway copy of the repository, in the run order above, with a 300-second per-script timeout. Of 62 scripts: 2 ran without error, 60 errored, 0 timed out, 0 could not run because one of its own dependencies is unavailable, 0 did not parse. Running against current package versions: a break can reflect version drift, which argues for pinning versions.

> compute_bootstrap_delta <- function(pred_a,
>                                     pred_b,
>                                     y_true,
>                                     B = 2000L,
>                                     seed = 42L,
>                                     virus_idx = NULL) {
>   err_a <- (pred_a - y_true)^2
>   err_b <- (pred_b - y_true)^2
> 
>   if (!is.null(virus_idx)) {
>     err_a <- err_a[, virus_idx, drop = FALSE]
>     err_b <- err_b[, virus_idx, drop = FALSE]
>   }
> 
>   delta <- err_a - err_b
>   observed_delta <- mean(delta)
>   mse_a <- mean(err_a)
> 
>   n <- nrow(delta)
>   set.seed(seed)
>   boot <- numeric(B)
>   for (b in seq_len(B)) {
>     idx <- sample.int(n, size = n, replace = TRUE)
>     boot[b] <- mean(delta[idx, , drop = FALSE])
>   }
> 
>   ci <- as.numeric(stats::quantile(boot, probs = c(0.025, 0.975), names = FALSE, na.rm = TRUE))
> 
>   list(
>     delta_mse = observed_delta,
>     delta_pct = 100 * observed_delta / mse_a,
>     ci_low = ci[1],
>     ci_high = ci[2],
>     prob_delta_gt0 = mean(boot > 0),
>     p_boot = mean(boot <= 0),
>     boot = boot,
>     mse_a = mse_a
>   )
> }
> safe_quartile <- function(x) {
>   q <- stats::quantile(x, probs = c(0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1), na.rm = TRUE)
>   if (any(diff(q) == 0)) {
>     r <- rank(x, ties.method = "average")
>     q_r <- stats::quantile(r, probs = c(0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1), na.rm = TRUE)
>     return(cut(r,
>                breaks = q_r,
>                include.lowest = TRUE,
>                labels = c("Q1_low", "Q2", "Q3", "Q4_high")))
>   }
>   cut(x,

>       include.lowest = TRUE,

> hierarchical_clustering <- function(data, k, method = "complete") {
>   hc <- hclust(dist(data), method = method)
>   clusters <- cutree(hc, k = k)

>     clusters = clusters,

> kmeans_clustering <- function(data, k, nstart = 25, iter_max = 100) {
>   set.seed(123)  # For reproducibility
>   km <- kmeans(data, centers = k, nstart = nstart, iter.max = iter_max)
> 
>   return(list(
>     clusters = km$cluster,

> euclidean_assignment <- function(test_data, centroids) {
>   apply(test_data, 1, function(row) {
>     dists <- apply(centroids, 1, function(centroid) {
>       sum((row - centroid)^2)
>     })
>     which.min(dists)
>   })
> }
> assign_to_clusters <- function(k, train_data, train_clusters, test_data) {
>   # Calculate centroids from training data only
>   centroids <- sapply(1:k, function(cluster) {
>     colMeans(train_data[train_clusters == cluster, , drop = FALSE])
>   })
> 
>   # Assign test cells to nearest centroid
>   test_clusters <- euclidean_assignment(test_data, t(centroids))
> 
>   return(test_clusters)
> }
> flowsom_clustering <- function(data, k, xdim = NULL, ydim = NULL) {
>   if (!requireNamespace("FlowSOM", quietly = TRUE)) {
>     stop("FlowSOM package is required but not installed. Install with: invisible(NULL)")
>   }
> 
>   # Adaptive SOM grid size: make grid larger than k to allow meaningful meta-clustering
>   # Use a grid that gives us ~2-3x more SOM nodes than final clusters
>   # This allows the SOM to capture structure while k controls final granularity
>   if (is.null(xdim) || is.null(ydim)) {
>     target_nodes <- max(100, k * 3)  # At least 100 nodes, or 3x k
>     grid_size <- ceiling(sqrt(target_nodes))
>     xdim <- grid_size
>     ydim <- grid_size
>   }
> 
>   # FlowSOM expects a flowFrame or matrix
>   # Build SOM
>   set.seed(123)
>   fsom <- FlowSOM::FlowSOM(data,
>                            colsToUse = 1:ncol(data),
>                            xdim = xdim,
>                            ydim = ydim,
>                            nClus = k,
>                            seed = 123)
> 
>   # Get cluster assignments
>   clusters <- FlowSOM::GetClusters(fsom)
> 
>   return(list(
>     clusters = clusters,
>     model = fsom,
>     method = "flowsom",
>     som_grid_size = c(xdim, ydim)
>   ))
> }
> leiden_clustering <- function(data, k, n_neighbors = 30, resolution = NULL) {
>   # Build kNN graph
>   # For large datasets, prefer RANN for speed and compatibility.
>   if (requireNamespace("RANN", quietly = TRUE)) {
>     # RANN includes the query point as the nearest neighbor.
>     knn_result <- RANN::nn2(data = data, query = data, k = n_neighbors + 1)
>     nn_index <- knn_result$nn.idx[, -1, drop = FALSE]
>     edges <- cbind(
>       rep(1:nrow(data), each = n_neighbors),
>       as.vector(t(nn_index))
>     )
>   } else if (requireNamespace("FNN", quietly = TRUE)) {
>     knn_result <- FNN::get.knn(data, k = n_neighbors)
>     edges <- cbind(
>       rep(1:nrow(data), each = n_neighbors),
>       as.vector(t(knn_result$nn.index))
>     )
>   } else {
>     # Fallback: simple distance-based
>     dists <- as.matrix(dist(data))
>     edges <- c()
>     for (i in 1:nrow(data)) {
>       neighbors <- order(dists[i, ])[2:(n_neighbors + 1)]
>       edges <- rbind(edges, cbind(i, neighbors))
>     }
>   }
> 
>   # Create igraph
>   g <- graph_from_edgelist(edges, directed = FALSE)
>   g <- simplify(g)  # Remove duplicates
> 
>   # If resolution not provided, estimate from desired k
>   if (is.null(resolution)) {
>     # Heuristic: resolution ≈ k / (n_nodes / 10)
>     # This is approximate and may need tuning
>     resolution <- k / (nrow(data) / 100)
>     resolution <- max(0.1, min(resolution, 2.0))  # Bound it
>   }
> 
>   # Perform Leiden clustering
>   set.seed(123)
>   leiden_result <- cluster_leiden(g,
>                                   resolution = resolution,
>                                   objective_function = "modularity")
> 
>   clusters <- membership(leiden_result)
> 
>   # Check if we got approximately k clusters
>   n_clusters_found <- length(unique(clusters))
>   if (abs(n_clusters_found - k) > k * 0.3) {
>     warning(sprintf("Leiden found %d clusters, target was %d. Consider adjusting resolution parameter.",
>                     n_clusters_found, k))
>   }
> 
>   return(list(
>     clusters = clusters,
>     model = leiden_result,
>     n_clusters_found = n_clusters_found,
>     resolution = resolution,
>     method = "leiden"
>   ))
> }
> perform_clustering <- function(data, k, method = "hierarchical", ...) {
>   if (method == "hierarchical") {
>     return(hierarchical_clustering(data, k, ...))
>   } else if (method == "kmeans") {
>     return(kmeans_clustering(data, k, ...))
>   } else if (method == "flowsom") {
>     return(flowsom_clustering(data, k, ...))
>   } else if (method == "leiden") {
>     return(leiden_clustering(data, k, ...))
>   } else {
>     stop("Unknown clustering method: ", method)
>   }
> }

> create_group_folds <- function(p_ids,
>                                group_ids,
>                                k,
>                                seed,
>                                strata = NULL) {
>   stopifnot(length(p_ids) > 0)
>   if (is.null(names(group_ids))) {
>     stop("group_ids must be a named vector keyed by p_id.")
>   }
>   group_ids <- as.character(group_ids[p_ids])
>   if (any(is.na(group_ids))) stop("group_ids missing for some p_ids.")
> 
>   groups <- unique(group_ids)
>   if (length(groups) < k) {
>     stop("Too few groups (", length(groups), ") for k=", k, " folds.")
>   }
> 
>   if (!is.null(strata)) {
>     if (is.null(names(strata))) names(strata) <- p_ids
>     strata <- strata[p_ids]
>     group_strata <- tapply(strata, group_ids, function(x) x[which(!is.na(x))[1]])
>     group_strata <- factor(group_strata[groups])
>     set.seed(seed)
>     fold_groups <- caret::createFolds(group_strata, k = k, list = TRUE)
>   } else {
>     set.seed(seed)
>     fold_groups <- caret::createFolds(seq_along(groups), k = k, list = TRUE)
>   }
> 
>   lapply(fold_groups, function(g_idx) {
>     test_groups <- groups[g_idx]
>     which(group_ids %in% test_groups)
>   })
> }
> as_pred_matrix <- function(pred, colnames_out) {

>     pred <- pred[, , 1, drop = FALSE]
>     dim(pred) <- dim(pred)[1:2]
>   }
>   pred <- as.matrix(pred)
>   colnames(pred) <- colnames_out
>   pred
> }
> fit_predict_multitask_en <- function(X_train,
>                                      Y_train,
>                                      X_test,
>                                      seed,
>                                      alpha = 0.5,
>                                      n_cores = 1,
>                                      foldid = NULL,
>                                      lambda_rule = c("lambda.min", "lambda.1se")) {
>   lambda_rule <- match.arg(lambda_rule)
>   set.seed(seed)
>   cv_args <- list(
>     x = X_train,
>     y = Y_train,
>     family = "mgaussian",
>     alpha = alpha,
>     standardize = TRUE,
>     parallel = n_cores > 1
>   )
>   if (!is.null(foldid)) {
>     cv_args$foldid <- foldid
>   } else {
>     cv_args$nfolds <- 5L
>   }
>   cvfit <- do.call(cv.glmnet, cv_args)
>   s_val <- if (lambda_rule == "lambda.min") cvfit$lambda.min else cvfit$lambda.1se

>     colnames(Y_train)
>   )
>   list(pred_train = pred_train, pred_test = pred_test, model = cvfit)
> }
> transform_counts <- function(meta_counts, method = c("log_count", "proportion", "clr")) {
>   method <- match.arg(method)
>   if (method == "log_count") {
>     return(as.matrix(log(meta_counts + 1)))
>   }
>   if (method == "proportion") {
>     row_sums <- rowSums(meta_counts)
>     row_sums[row_sums == 0] <- 1  # avoid division by zero
>     return(as.matrix(meta_counts / row_sums))
>   }
>   if (method == "clr") {
>     # Centered log-ratio: log(x_i / geometric_mean(x))
>     props <- meta_counts + 0.5  # pseudocount for zeros
>     log_props <- log(props)
>     geo_means <- rowMeans(log_props)
>     return(as.matrix(log_props - geo_means))
>   }
> }
> fit_ols_baseline <- function(X_train, y_train, X_test) {
>   df_train <- data.frame(X_train, y = y_train, check.names = FALSE)
>   model <- lm(y ~ ., data = df_train)
>   pred_train <- predict(model, newdata = data.frame(X_train, check.names = FALSE))
>   pred_test <- predict(model, newdata = data.frame(X_test, check.names = FALSE))
>   list(pred_train = as.numeric(pred_train),
>        pred_test = as.numeric(pred_test),
>        model = model)
> }

>   pred_train <- matrix(NA_real_, nrow = nrow(X_train), ncol = length(virus_names))

>   }
>   list(pred_train = pred_train, pred_test = pred_test)

> build_ratio_feature_matrices <- function(train_meta_counts,
>                                          test_meta_counts = NULL,
>                                          max_ratio_features = 561,
>                                          selector = c("variance", "all")) {
>   selector <- match.arg(selector)
> 
>   train_log <- log(train_meta_counts + 1)
>   test_log <- if (is.null(test_meta_counts)) NULL else log(test_meta_counts + 1)
>   k <- ncol(train_log)
>   if (k < 2) {
>     empty_train <- matrix(numeric(0), nrow = nrow(train_log), ncol = 0)
>     rownames(empty_train) <- rownames(train_log)
>     empty_test <- NULL
>     if (!is.null(test_log)) {
>       empty_test <- matrix(numeric(0), nrow = nrow(test_log), ncol = 0)
>       rownames(empty_test) <- rownames(test_log)
>     }
>     return(list(train = empty_train, test = empty_test, pairs = matrix(numeric(0), ncol = 2)))
>   }
> 
>   pairs <- t(utils::combn(k, 2))
>   n_pairs <- nrow(pairs)
>   keep_idx <- seq_len(n_pairs)
> 
>   if (selector == "variance" && is.finite(max_ratio_features) && max_ratio_features < n_pairs) {
>     var_scores <- apply(pairs, 1, function(idx) {
>       stats::var(train_log[, idx[1]] - train_log[, idx[2]], na.rm = TRUE)
>     })
>     keep_idx <- order(var_scores, decreasing = TRUE)[seq_len(max_ratio_features)]
>   }
> 
>   if (selector == "all" && is.finite(max_ratio_features) && max_ratio_features < n_pairs) {
>     keep_idx <- seq_len(max_ratio_features)
>   }
> 
>   keep_pairs <- pairs[sort(keep_idx), , drop = FALSE]
>   if (nrow(keep_pairs) == 0) {
>     empty_train <- matrix(numeric(0), nrow = nrow(train_log), ncol = 0)
>     rownames(empty_train) <- rownames(train_log)
>     empty_test <- NULL
>     if (!is.null(test_log)) {
>       empty_test <- matrix(numeric(0), nrow = nrow(test_log), ncol = 0)
>       rownames(empty_test) <- rownames(test_log)
>     }
>     return(list(train = empty_train, test = empty_test, pairs = keep_pairs))
>   }
> 
>   ratio_names <- paste0("ratio_c", keep_pairs[, 1], "_c", keep_pairs[, 2])
>   ratio_train <- sapply(seq_len(nrow(keep_pairs)), function(i) {
>     idx <- keep_pairs[i, ]
>     train_log[, idx[1]] - train_log[, idx[2]]
>   })
>   ratio_train <- as.matrix(ratio_train)
>   colnames(ratio_train) <- ratio_names
>   rownames(ratio_train) <- rownames(train_log)
> 
>   ratio_test <- NULL
>   if (!is.null(test_log)) {
